Figure 17:
Boolean Translation After Box Inference

Following the spirit from Chapter
2
, the boxes are
reconstructed by a type directed algorithm that by construction
produces well typed expressions as a result. The AST produced by this
phase, is complete, and can be type-checked without having to infer or
complete any type information. A small type-checker would make the
trusted computing base of the system small. Similarly, having implicit
parameter reconstruction would make writing programs easier to write.
Both aspects are planned additions to the Orca prototype.

6
Related Work

The idea of embedding a specification framework in a computational

\lambda
-calculus using a modality was first presented
in
(
Despeyroux et al. 1997
)
where they embed a simply-typed version of LF
in a simply typed lambda calculus extended with a box modality based
on that from S4. As mentioned, in Orca the reasoning framework is the
logical framework LF
(
Harper et al. 1993
)
and the computational
framework is a dependently typed system
(
Martin-Löf 1982
)
. This
presents a very first step towards answering the long standing
question of combining HOAS and dependent types.

Moreover, Orca can be seen as an extension of the Beluga
language
(
Pientka and Cave 2015
)
. As such it uses the logical framework
LF to represent syntax and judgments using higher-order abstract
syntax (HOAS). However, it extends Beluga’s first order reasoning
language to a dependently typed system where computations can be
embedded in specifications. The Twelf system
(
Pfenning and Schürmann 1999
)

also implements LF where they implement computation using logic
programming instead of a functional framework. The Delphin
system
(
Poswolsky and Schürmann 2008
)
manipulates LF objects using
functional programs in a similar way to Beluga but without support for
inductive types or contextual types.

Other approaches that use a two-level system with specifications and
reasoning are the Abella prover
(
Baelde et al. 2014
)
and
Hybrid framework
(
Felty and Momigliano 2012
)
, these two systems provide a
specification logic on top of a proof-theoretic reasoning logic.
Abella is a standalone proof assistant, while Hybrid can be
implemented on existing proof assistants, implementations for Coq and
Isabelle exist.

Traditionally, implementing HOAS in systems without a specification
framework is troublesome because of the positivity restriction for
inductive types. To overcome this problem, weaker forms of HOAS use an
abstract type to represent variables and obtain a strictly positive
type. Examples of this are the work of
Chlipala 2008

and
Despeyroux and Hirschowitz 1994
. Weaker forms of HOAS
are convenient but they do not get substitution from
\beta
-reduction
in the host language and make dealing with open terms less convenient.

Another option is to implement binders using de Bruijn indices. A
convenient way is using well-scoped de Bruijn
indices
(
Altenkirch 1993
)
(and later extended to intrinsically
typed terms in
(
Benton et al. 2012a
)
), where substitution is
implemented as an inductive data type and its structural properties
need to be proved manually. There are implementations of de Bruijn
indices that provide and facilitate proving the required lemmas. One
example is Autosubst
(
Schäfer et al. 2015
)
that implements a decision
procedure to compare terms with explicit substitutions. The Nameless
Painless
(
Pouillard 2011
)
approach is based on de Bruijn
indices, but it simplifies the arithmetic reasoning by hiding the
numbers in an abstract “world” representation. Finally,
GMeta
(
Lee et al. 2012
)
simplifies implementing first-order binding
representations (e.g.: de Bruijn indices) using generic programming to
provide proofs for the lemmas about binders.

More recently,
Allais et al. 2017
propose a way of encoding syntax
(as opposed to syntax and judgments) in Agda using a generic type and
scope preserving semantics. This allows them to prove lemmas about the
semantics and then reuse them to implement renaming, substitution,
normalization by evaluation and CPS transformations.

Finally, a possible approach is to use nominal
logic
(
Pitts 2003
)
that uses an infinite set of atoms (abstract
names) to replace the position based approach. These ideas have been
implemented in Nominal
(
Urban 2008
)
package of
Isabelle
(
Nipkow et al. 2002
)
.

Finally, thanks to its specification framework, Orca is
well-positioned to implement meta-programming in dependently typed
systems where syntax can be reflected into LF objects where
computational functions can manipulate them. There is interest in
doing this in existing systems as a way of implementing tactics and
elaboration, for example the MTAC
(
Ziliani et al. 2015
)
system for Coq,
or elaboration reflection
(
Christiansen and Brady 2016
)
for Idris
implement meta-programming (The Agda proof assistant also implements
Idris-style reflection). What Orca offers to meta-programming is the
ability of processing structures with binders in a type safe and
convenient way, instead, for example, of the de Bruijn representation
that the Idris reflection offers.

7
Conclusion

In this chapter we describe Orca, as an idea and a prototype that
implements a Martin-Löf style type theory with a specification
language based on the logical framework LF. This language can be
thought of as a dependently typed Beluga, and thus continues the theme
of cetacean inspired names (after Beluga and Delphin). The design of
Orca can also be seen as a next step extending the design of the
Babybel system from Chapter
3
to total languages and
full LF and thus continues that line of work. However, this chapter
does not address the full spectrum of possibilities and questions
about Orca. This should not be seen as a limitation but as an
aspiration. Exploring the meta-theory of the system, its expressivity
and the fields where it will be applicable is all exciting future
work. Some of this work is already in progress, particularly the
meta-theory and improving and extending the prototype with new ideas.
However, the current state of affairs is promising, we show how Orca
can interleave computation and specifications, and how this saves some
lemmas that would be necessary in systems that need to represent
specification computation as relations. Moreover, the Orca
distribution contains several examples that hint to the power of
reasoning not only about specifications and inductive types like in
Beluga, but also about functions. Some of these examples are: a small
type preservation proof, the translation we used as an example here, a
proof that a particular computation preservers types (illustrated with
the simplest computation function, the copy function), a conversion
between two styles of operational semantics and several small programs
that we use as the beginnings of a test harness. In conclusion, Orca
represents a new bold first step in the reasoning about open objects
and offers a path to explore extensions to the ideas presented in this
chapter.

Chapter 5
Conclusion

In this thesis, after introducing the problem of reasoning about open
terms using a HOAS representation, we show how to simplify writing
these programs using inference of omitted arguments, how to write
programs by integrating contextual types and HOAS with existing
programming languages, and finally we present the Orca system that
does the first step towards integrating a Martin-Löf’s style type
theory with contextual types and the logical framework LF, this allows
for the interleaving of specifications and proofs/computations.
Therefore, Orca, once the meta-theory is proven and the totality check
implemented, allows for proofs about meta-theoretic properties of
specifications together with proofs about computations which allows
the user to prove properties of their computations over
specifications. This provides for the seamless combination of programs
and proofs.

1
Future Work

1
Implicit Parameter Reconstruction

Implicit parameter reconstruction makes our lives easier, by allowing
us to concentrate in what is important. Reconstruction only fills in
parts that are forced by the surrounding program. As future work, one
would like to extend the kinds of information that can be inferred.
Furthermore, as the current algorithm works for indexed type systems,
it would need to be extended for full dependent types to support
reconstruction of Orca programs. Having a formally specified type
reconstruction for Orca will dramatically improve its ergonomics.
Another interesting approach would be to specify reconstruction as
form elaboration reflection
(
Christiansen and Brady 2016
)
that would allow
for reconstruction to be done in Orca code itself. Orca’s
specification framework should make for a great target for this
reflection mechanism because the LF framework could be used to
represent the program being elaborated.

2
Contextual Types and Programming Languages

In the future, we plan to implement our approach also in other
languages. In particular, it would be natural to implement our
approach in Haskell. GHC Haskell offers the required syntax extension
mechanism and its more powerful type system offers interesting
possibilities. With regards to the implementation, the PPX syntax
extension mechanism is a bit limited (e.g.: the syntax of type
annotations cannot be extended), it would be interesting to either
extend PPX or to replace PPX with CamlP4 that would allow for a more
seamless syntax extension. And finally, while the current features of
Babybel allow for many interesting use cases, it would be interesting
to develop more substantial use cases to both validate the convenience
our approach and to possibly justify new features that a larger
program might require. For example, implementing the compiler for a
small language would neatly showcase the use of the syntactic
framework.

3
Contextual Types and Type Theory

This chapter represents an open path to future research, which is
another way to say that there are lots of open questions regarding
this subjects. So far, there is no meta-theory for the core calculus
we presented, as future work it would be important to show that
normalization is preserved when extending a dependently typed calculus
with the logical framework LF using contextual types in the way
described in this chapter
1
1

1

There is ongoing work on draft of
meta-theory for this
. Also on the side of the theory, certain
needed features are missing to make it more expressive, like
substitution variables
(
Cave and Pientka 2013
)
. Finally, inspired by the
Babybel theory where contexts are erased at run-time, in this calculus
contexts are run-time irrelevant. This means that properties about
contexts need to be established using inductive predicates (e.g.: the

rel
predicate in the example from
Section
2
). Adding Beluga style context schemas would
allow for more powerful use of contexts.

On the side of the Orca prototype, the immediate future work is
mechanically making sure that the functions are total, that is
implementing coverage and termination checking. Implementing coverage
would extend ideas from the Beluga system
(
Pientka and Dunfield 2010a
)
and
for termination many choices are possible. The author would like to
explore the idea of sized types
(
Hughes et al. 1996
)
and

(
Abel 2006
)
. And from here the possibilities expand, as
Orca can be used as a vehicle for research in this area. Examples of
this are: adding generic judgments as in the work by

Miller and Tiu 2005
to have Abella
(
Gacek 2008
)
and
Delphin style introduction of fresh names, and exploring co-inductive
types with co-patterns.

Chapter 6
Proof of Soundness of Reconstruction

We begin with some lemmas that we will use to establish the main result:

Lemma 1

(Implicit parameter instantiation)
.

Let’s
consider the judgment:

\Theta;\Delta;\Gamma\vdash E{\,\mathrel{:}\,}T\rightsquigarrow E_{1}{\,\mathrel{:}\,}T_{1}/\Theta_{1}
,
where
\Theta_{1}
is a weakening of
\Theta
.

We want to prove that, if
\rho_{g}
is a grounding instantiation such as
\cdot\vdash\rho_{g}{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\Theta_{1}

where we split
\rho_{g}=\rho_{g}^{\prime},\rho_{g}^{\prime\prime}
and
\cdot\vdash\rho_{g}^{\prime}{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\Theta
and

\cdot;\llbracket\rho_{g}^{\prime}\rrbracket\Delta;\llbracket\rho_{g}^{\prime}\rrbracket\Gamma\vdash\llbracket\rho_{g}^{\prime}\rrbracket E{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\llbracket\rho_{g}^{\prime}\rrbracket T
then

\cdot;\llbracket\rho_{g}\rrbracket\Delta;\llbracket\rho_{g}\rrbracket\Gamma\vdash\llbracket\rho_{g}\rrbracket E_{1}{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\llbracket\rho_{g}\rrbracket T_{1}
.

Proof.

The proof follows by induction on the rules of the judgment where
the base case for
el-impl-done
is trivial and the inductive step for

el-impl
has also a very direct proof.
∎

Lemma 2

(Pattern elaboration)
.

1.

If
\Theta;\Delta\vdash pat\rightsquigarrow\Pi\Delta_{1};\Gamma_{1}.Pat{\,\mathrel{:}\,}T/\Theta_{1};\rho_{1}
and

\rho_{r}
is a further refinement substitution, such as
\Theta_{2}\vdash\rho_{r}{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\Theta_{1}
and

\epsilon
is a ground lifting substitution, such as
\Delta_{i}\vdash\epsilon{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\Theta_{1}
then

\Delta_{i},\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket\Delta_{1};\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket\Gamma_{1}\vdash\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket Pat\mathrel{\Leftarrow}\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket T
.

2.

If
\Theta;\Delta\vdash pat:{T}\rightsquigarrow\Pi\Delta_{1};\Gamma_{1}.Pat/\Theta_{1};\rho_{1}
and

\rho_{r}
is a further refinement substitution, such as
\Theta_{2}\vdash\rho_{r}{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\Theta_{1}
and

\epsilon
is a ground lifting substitution, such as
\Delta_{i}\vdash\epsilon{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\Theta_{1}
then

\Delta_{i},\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket\Delta_{1};\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket\Gamma_{1}\vdash\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket Pat\mathrel{\Leftarrow}\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket T
.

3.

If
\Theta;\Delta\vdash\overrightarrow{pat}:{T}\rightsquigarrow\Pi\Delta_{1};\Gamma_{1}.\overrightarrow{Pat}\mathrel{\rangle}S/\Theta_{1};\rho_{1}
and

\rho_{r}
is a further refinement substitution, such as
\Theta_{2}\vdash\rho_{r}{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\Theta_{1}
and

\epsilon
is a ground lifting substitution, such as
\Delta_{i}\vdash\epsilon{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\Theta_{1}
then

\Delta_{i},\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket\Delta_{1};\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket\Gamma_{1}\vdash\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket\overrightarrow{Pat}\mathrel{\Leftarrow}\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket T\mathrel{\rangle}\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket S
.

Proof.

By simultaneous induction on the first derivation.

For (1):

Case

\mathcal{D}:\Theta;\Delta\vdash\textbf{c}\,\overrightarrow{pat}\rightsquigarrow\Pi\Delta_{1};\Gamma_{1}.\textbf{c}\,\overrightarrow{Pat}{\,\mathrel{:}\,}S/\Theta_{1};\rho_{1}

\Sigma(\textbf{c})=T

\Theta;\Delta\vdash\overrightarrow{pat}:{T}\rightsquigarrow\Pi\Delta_{1};\Gamma_{1}.\overrightarrow{Pat}\mathrel{\rangle}S/\Theta_{1};\rho_{1}

by assumption

\Delta_{i},\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket\Delta_{1};\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket\Gamma_{1}\vdash\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket\overrightarrow{Pat}\mathrel{\Leftarrow}\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket T\mathrel{\rangle}\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket S

by i.h. (3)

Note that types in the signature (i.e.
\Sigma
) are ground so
\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket T=T

\Delta_{i},\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket\Delta_{1};\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket\Gamma_{1}\vdash\textbf{c}\,(\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket\overrightarrow{Pat})\mathrel{\Leftarrow}\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket S

by
t-pcon
.

\Delta_{i},\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket\Delta_{1};\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket\Gamma_{1}\vdash\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket(\textbf{c}\,\overrightarrow{Pat})\mathrel{\Leftarrow}\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket S

by properties of substitution

which is what we wanted to show.

For (2):

Case

\mathcal{E}:\Theta;\Delta\vdash x:{T}\rightsquigarrow\Pi\Delta_{1}~;~\underbrace{x{\,\mathrel{:}\,}T}_{\Gamma_{1}}.x~/~\Theta;{\texttt{id}}(\Theta)

\Gamma_{1}(x)=T

by
x
being the only variable in
\Gamma_{1}

\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket\Gamma_{1}=\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket\Gamma_{1}T

by applying
\epsilon
and
\rho_{r}
to
\Delta_{1}
,
\Gamma_{1}
and
T

\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket\Delta_{1};\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket\Gamma_{1}\vdash x\mathrel{\Leftarrow}\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket T

by rule
t-pvar

which is what we wanted to prove

For (3):

Case

\mathcal{F}:\Theta;\Delta\vdash pat~~\overrightarrow{pat}:{T_{1}\to T_{2}}\rightsquigarrow\Pi\Delta_{2};\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}.(\llbracket\rho^{\prime}\rrbracket Pat)~~\overrightarrow{Pat}\mathrel{\rangle}S/\Theta_{2};\rho_{2}\circ\rho_{1}

\Theta;\Delta\vdash pat:{T_{1}}\rightsquigarrow\Pi\Delta_{1};\Gamma_{1}.Pat/\Theta_{1};\rho_{1}

\Theta_{1};\Delta_{1}\vdash\overrightarrow{pat}:{\llbracket\rho\rrbracket T_{2}}\rightsquigarrow\Pi\Delta_{2};\Gamma_{2}.\overrightarrow{Pat}\mathrel{\rangle}S/\Theta_{2};\rho_{2}

by assumption

\Theta_{2}\vdash\rho_{2}{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\Theta_{1}
by invariant of rule

\Theta_{3}\vdash\rho_{3}\circ\rho_{2}{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\Theta_{1}

(further refinement substitution)
by composition

\Delta_{i}\vdash\epsilon{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\Theta_{3}
lifting substitution

\Delta_{i},\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\circ\rho_{2}\rrbracket\Delta_{1};\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\circ\rho_{2}\rrbracket\Gamma_{1}\vdash\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\circ\rho_{2}\rrbracket Pat\mathrel{\Leftarrow}\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\circ\rho_{2}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket T_{1}

by i.h. on (1).
(\dagger)

\Delta_{i},\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\rrbracket\Delta_{2};\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\rrbracket\Gamma_{2}\vdash\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\rrbracket\overrightarrow{Pat}\mathrel{\Leftarrow}\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\circ\rho_{2}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket T_{2}\mathrel{\rangle}\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\rrbracket S

by i.h. on (2)

we note that in pattern elaboration we have:

\Delta_{2}=\llbracket\rho_{2}\rrbracket\Delta_{1},\Delta^{\prime}_{2}

\Delta_{2}
is the context
\Delta_{1}
with the hole instantiation applied and
some extra assumptions (i.e.
\Delta^{\prime}_{2}
))).

and
\Gamma_{2}=\llbracket\rho_{2}\rrbracket\Gamma_{1},\Gamma^{\prime}_{2}

\Gamma_{2}
is the context
\Gamma_{1}
with the hole instantiation applied and
some extra assumptions (i.e.
\Gamma^{\prime}_{2}
).

and we can weaken
(\dagger)
to:

\Delta_{i},\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\circ\rho_{2}\rrbracket\Delta_{1},\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\rrbracket\Delta^{\prime}_{2};\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\circ\rho_{2}\rrbracket\Gamma_{1},\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\rrbracket\Gamma^{\prime}_{2}\vdash\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\circ\rho_{2}\rrbracket Pat\mathrel{\Leftarrow}\llbracket\rho\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\circ\rho_{2}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket T_{1}

\Delta_{i},\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\rrbracket\Delta_{2};\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\rrbracket\Gamma_{2}\vdash(\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\circ\rho_{2}\rrbracket Pat)(\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\rrbracket\overrightarrow{Pat})\mathrel{\Leftarrow}\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\circ\rho_{2}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket T_{1}\to\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\circ\rho_{2}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket T_{2}\mathrel{\rangle}\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\rrbracket S

by
t-sarr
.

\Delta_{i},\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\rrbracket\Delta_{2};\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\rrbracket\Gamma_{2}\vdash\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\rrbracket(\llbracket\rho_{2}\rrbracket Pat\,\overrightarrow{Pat})\mathrel{\Leftarrow}\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\circ\rho_{2}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket(T_{1}\to T_{2})\mathrel{\rangle}\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\rrbracket S

by properties of substitution

which is what we wanted to show.

Case

\mathcal{F}:\Theta;\Delta\vdash[c]\,\overrightarrow{pat}:{\Pi^{e}X{\,\mathrel{:}\,}U.\,T}\rightsquigarrow\Pi\Delta_{2};\Gamma_{2}.(\llbracket\rho_{1}\rrbracket[C])\,\overrightarrow{Pat}\mathrel{\rangle}S/\Theta_{2};\rho_{2}\circ\rho_{1}

\Theta;\Delta\vdash c:{U}\rightsquigarrow C/\Theta_{1};\Delta_{1};\rho_{1}

\Theta_{1};\Delta_{1}\vdash\overrightarrow{pat}:{[C/X]\llbracket\rho_{1}\rrbracket T}\rightsquigarrow\Pi\Delta_{2};\Gamma_{2}.\overrightarrow{Pat}\mathrel{\rangle}S/\Theta_{2};\rho_{2}

by property of the index language
(\dagger)

\Delta_{i},\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\rrbracket\Delta_{2};\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\rrbracket\Gamma_{2}\vdash\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\rrbracket\overrightarrow{Pat}\mathrel{\Leftarrow}\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\circ\rho_{2}\rrbracket([C/X]\llbracket\rho_{1}\rrbracket T)\mathrel{\rangle}\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\rrbracket S

\Delta_{2}=\llbracket\rho_{2}\rrbracket\Delta_{1},\Delta^{\prime}_{2}

\Delta_{2}
is the context
\Delta_{1}
with the hole instantiation applied and
some extra assumptions (i.e.
\Delta^{\prime}_{2}
).

and we can weaken
(\dagger)
to:

\Delta_{i},\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\circ\rho_{2}\rrbracket\Delta_{1},\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\rrbracket\Delta^{\prime}_{2}\vdash\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\circ\rho_{2}\rrbracket C\mathrel{\Leftarrow}\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\circ\rho_{2}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket U

Note that
\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\circ\rho_{2}\rrbracket([C/X]\llbracket\rho_{1}\rrbracket T)=[(\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\circ\rho_{2}\rrbracket C)/X](\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\circ\rho_{2}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket T)

by properties of substitution

\Delta_{i},\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\rrbracket\Delta_{2};\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\rrbracket\Gamma_{2}\vdash[\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\circ\rho_{2}\rrbracket C]\,(\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\rrbracket\overrightarrow{Pat})\mathrel{\Leftarrow}\Pi^{e}X{\,\mathrel{:}\,}(\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\circ\rho_{2}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket U).\,(\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\circ\rho_{2}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket T)\mathrel{\rangle}\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\rrbracket S

by
t-spi

\Delta_{i},\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\rrbracket\Delta_{2};\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\rrbracket\Gamma_{2}\vdash\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\rrbracket([\llbracket\rho_{2}\rrbracket C]\,\overrightarrow{Pat}))\mathrel{\Leftarrow}\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\circ\rho_{2}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket(\Pi^{e}X{\,\mathrel{:}\,}U.\,T)\mathrel{\rangle}\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{3}\rrbracket S

by properties of substitution

which is what we wanted to show.

Case

\mathcal{F}:\Theta;\Delta\vdash\overrightarrow{pat}:{\Pi^{i}X{\,\mathrel{:}\,}U.\,T}\rightsquigarrow\Pi\Delta_{1};\Gamma_{1}.(\llbracket\rho_{1}\rrbracket C)\,\overrightarrow{Pat}\mathrel{\rangle}S/\Theta_{1};\rho_{1}

\mathsf{genHole}\;(?Y:\Delta.U)=C

\Theta,?Y{\,\mathrel{:}\,}{\Delta.U};\Delta\vdash\overrightarrow{pat}:{[C/X]T}\rightsquigarrow\Pi\Delta^{\prime};\Gamma^{\prime}.\overrightarrow{Pat}/\Theta^{\prime};\rho\mathrel{\rangle}S

by assumption

\Theta,?Y{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\Delta.U;\Delta\vdash C\mathrel{\Leftarrow}U
by genhole invariant

\Delta_{i},\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket\Delta\vdash\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket C\mathrel{\Leftarrow}\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket U

applying substitutions
\epsilon,\rho_{r}and\rho_{1}

noting that
\Delta_{1}=\llbracket\rho_{1}\rrbracket\Delta,\Delta^{\prime}_{1}

\Delta_{i},\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket(\llbracket\rho_{1}\rrbracket\Delta,\Delta^{\prime}_{1})\vdash\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket C\mathrel{\Leftarrow}\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket U

by weakening

\Delta_{i},\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket\Delta_{1};\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket\overrightarrow{Pat}\mathrel{\Leftarrow}\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket[C/X]T\mathrel{\rangle}\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket S

by i.h. (3)

\Delta_{i},\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket\Delta_{1};\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket\overrightarrow{Pat}\mathrel{\Leftarrow}[\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket C/X](\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket T)\mathrel{\rangle}\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket S

by properties of substitution

\Delta_{i},\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket\Delta_{1};\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash[\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket C]\,\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket\overrightarrow{Pat}\mathrel{\Leftarrow}\Pi^{i}X{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket U.\,(\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket T)\mathrel{\rangle}\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket S

by
t-spi

\Delta_{i},\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket\Delta_{1};\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket[\llbracket\rho_{1}\rrbracket C]\,\overrightarrow{Pat}\mathrel{\Leftarrow}\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket(\Pi^{i}X{\,\mathrel{:}\,}U.\,T)\mathrel{\rangle}\llbracket\epsilon\rrbracket\llbracket\rho_{r}\rrbracket S

by properties of substitution

which is what we wanted to show

∎

We can now prove the theorem:

Theorem 6..1

(Soundness)
.

1.

If
\Theta;\Delta;\Gamma\vdash\Lbag e\mathrel{;}\theta\Rbag:{T}\rightsquigarrow E/\Theta_{1};\rho_{1}

then for any grounding hole instantiation
\rho_{g}
s.t.

\cdot\vdash\rho_{g}:\Theta_{1}
and
\rho_{0}=\rho_{g}\circ\rho_{1}
, we have

\llbracket\rho_{0}\rrbracket\Delta;\llbracket\rho_{0}\rrbracket\Gamma\vdash\llbracket\rho_{g}\rrbracket E\mathrel{\Leftarrow}\llbracket\rho_{0}\rrbracket T
.

2.

If
\Theta;\Delta;\Gamma\vdash\Lbag e\mathrel{;}\theta\Rbag\rightsquigarrow E{\,\mathrel{:}\,}T/\Theta_{1};\rho_{1}

then for any grounding hole instantiation
\rho_{g}
s.t.
\cdot\vdash\rho_{g}:\Theta_{1}
and
\rho_{0}=\rho_{g}\circ\rho_{1}
, we have

\llbracket\rho_{0}\rrbracket\Delta;\llbracket\rho_{0}\rrbracket\Gamma\vdash\llbracket\rho_{g}\rrbracket E\mathrel{\Rightarrow}\llbracket\rho_{g}\rrbracket T
.

3.

If
\Delta;\Gamma\vdash\Lbag pat\mapsto e\mathrel{;}\theta\Rbag:{S\to T}\rightsquigarrow\Pi\Delta^{\prime};\Gamma^{\prime}.Pat:\theta^{\prime}\mapsto E

then
\Delta;\Gamma\vdash\Pi\Delta^{\prime};\Gamma^{\prime}.Pat:\theta^{\prime}\mapsto E\mathrel{\Leftarrow}S\to T
.

Proof.

By simultaneous induction on the first derivation.

For (1):

Case

{\mathcal{D}}:\Theta;\Delta;\Gamma\vdash\Lbag\mbox{{{case}}}\,e\,\mbox{{{of}}}\,\overrightarrow{b}\mathrel{;}\theta\Rbag:{T}\rightsquigarrow\mbox{{{case}}}\,E\,\mbox{{{of}}}\,\overrightarrow{B}/\Theta^{\prime};\rho

\Theta;\Delta;\Gamma\vdash\Lbag e\mathrel{;}\theta\Rbag\rightsquigarrow E{\,\mathrel{:}\,}S/\cdot;\rho

by inversion on
el-case

\llbracket\rho\rrbracket\Delta;\llbracket\rho\rrbracket\Gamma\vdash\Lbag\overrightarrow{b}\mathrel{;}\llbracket\rho\rrbracket\theta\Rbag:{S\to\llbracket\rho\rrbracket T}\rightsquigarrow\overrightarrow{B}

by inversion on
el-case

for any grounding hole inst.
\rho^{\prime}
we have

\llbracket\rho\rrbracket\Delta;\llbracket\rho\rrbracket\Gamma\vdash E\mathrel{\Rightarrow}S

by I.H. noting
\rho^{\prime}=\cdot
and
\rho^{\prime}\circ\rho=\rho

\llbracket\rho\rrbracket\Delta;\llbracket\rho\rrbracket\Gamma\vdash B{\,\mathrel{:}\,}S\to\llbracket\rho\rrbracket T

for every branch by (3)

\llbracket\rho\rrbracket\Delta;\llbracket\rho\rrbracket\Gamma\vdash\mbox{{{case}}}\,E\,\mbox{{{of}}}\,\overrightarrow{B}\mathrel{\Leftarrow}\llbracket\rho\rrbracket T

by
t-case

Note that because
E
is ground then the only grounding hole to instantiate is the empty substitution.

Case

\mathcal{D}:\Theta;\Delta;\Gamma\vdash\Lbag{\texttt{fn}}\,x\Rightarrow e\mathrel{;}\theta\Rbag:{T_{1}\to T_{2}}\rightsquigarrow{\texttt{fn}}\,x\Rightarrow E/\Theta_{1};\rho_{1}

\Theta;\Delta;\Gamma,x{\,\mathrel{:}\,}T_{1}\vdash\Lbag e\mathrel{;}\theta\Rbag:{T_{2}}\rightsquigarrow E/\Theta_{1};\rho_{1}

by assumption

for any grounding hole inst.
\rho_{g}
we have:

\llbracket\rho_{0}\rrbracket\Delta;\llbracket\rho_{o}\rrbracket(\Gamma,x{\,\mathrel{:}\,}T_{1})\vdash\llbracket\rho_{g}\rrbracket E\mathrel{\Leftarrow}\llbracket\rho_{0}\rrbracket T_{2}

by i.h. (1) with
\rho_{0}=\rho_{g}\circ\rho_{1}

\llbracket\rho_{0}\rrbracket\Delta;(\llbracket\rho_{o}\rrbracket\Gamma),x{\,\mathrel{:}\,}(\llbracket\rho_{0}\rrbracket T_{1})\vdash\llbracket\rho_{g}\rrbracket E\mathrel{\Leftarrow}\llbracket\rho_{0}\rrbracket T_{2}

by properties of substitution

\llbracket\rho_{0}\rrbracket\Delta;\llbracket\rho_{o}\rrbracket\Gamma\vdash{\texttt{fn}}\,x\Rightarrow(\llbracket\rho_{g}\rrbracket E)\mathrel{\Leftarrow}(\llbracket\rho_{0}\rrbracket T_{1})\to(\llbracket\rho_{0}\rrbracket T_{2})

by
t-fn

\llbracket\rho_{0}\rrbracket\Delta;\llbracket\rho_{o}\rrbracket\Gamma\vdash\llbracket\rho_{g}\rrbracket({\texttt{fn}}\,x\Rightarrow E)\mathrel{\Leftarrow}\llbracket\rho_{0}\rrbracket(T_{1})\to T_{2})

by properties of substitution

which is what we wanted to show

Case

\mathcal{D}:\Theta;\Delta;\Gamma\vdash\Lbag\mbox{{{mlam}}}\,X\Rightarrow e\mathrel{;}\theta\Rbag:{\Pi^{e}X{\,\mathrel{:}\,}U.\,T}\rightsquigarrow\mbox{{{mlam}}}\,X\Rightarrow E/\Theta_{1};\rho_{1}

\Theta;\Delta,X{\,\mathrel{:}\,}U;\Gamma\vdash\Lbag e\mathrel{;}\theta,X/X\Rbag:{T}\rightsquigarrow E/\Theta_{1};\rho_{1}

by assumption

for any grounding hole inst.
\rho_{g}
we have:
\llbracket\rho_{0}\rrbracket(\Delta,X{\,\mathrel{:}\,}U);\llbracket\rho_{0}\rrbracket\Gamma\vdash\llbracket\rho_{g}\rrbracket E\mathrel{\Leftarrow}\llbracket\rho_{o}\rrbracket T

by i.h.(1) with
\rho_{0}=\rho_{g}\circ\rho_{1}

\llbracket\rho_{o}\rrbracket\Delta,X{\,\mathrel{:}\,}(\llbracket\rho_{0}\rrbracket U);\llbracket\rho_{0}\rrbracket\Gamma\vdash\llbracket\rho_{g}\rrbracket E\mathrel{\Leftarrow}\llbracket\rho_{o}\rrbracket T

by properties of subst

\llbracket\rho_{o}\rrbracket\Delta;\llbracket\rho_{0}\rrbracket\Gamma\vdash\mbox{{{mlam}}}\,X\Rightarrow\llbracket\rho_{g}\rrbracket E\mathrel{\Leftarrow}\Pi^{e}X{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\llbracket\rho_{0}\rrbracket U.\,(\llbracket\rho_{o}\rrbracket T)

by
t-mlam

\llbracket\rho_{o}\rrbracket\Delta;\llbracket\rho_{0}\rrbracket\Gamma\vdash\llbracket\rho_{g}\rrbracket\mbox{{{mlam}}}\,X\Rightarrow E\mathrel{\Leftarrow}\llbracket\rho_{0}\rrbracket\Pi^{e}X{\,\mathrel{:}\,}U.\,T

by properties of substitution

which is what we wanted to show

Case

\mathcal{D}:\Theta;\Delta;\Gamma\vdash\Lbag e\mathrel{;}\theta\Rbag:{\Pi^{i}X{\,\mathrel{:}\,}U.\,T}\rightsquigarrow\mbox{{{mlam}}}\,X\Rightarrow E/\Theta_{1};\rho_{1}

this case follows the same structure as the previous

Case

\mathcal{D}:\Theta;\Delta;\Gamma\vdash\Lbag[c]\mathrel{;}\theta\Rbag:{[U]}\rightsquigarrow[C]/\Theta_{1};\rho_{1}

for any grounding inst.
\rho_{g}

by properties of the index language and
\rho_{0}=\rho_{g}\circ\rho_{1}

\llbracket\rho_{0}\rrbracket\Delta;\llbracket\rho_{0}\rrbracket\Gamma\vdash\llbracket\rho_{g}\rrbracket[C]\mathrel{\Leftarrow}\llbracket\rho_{0}\rrbracket[U]

by
t-box
and properties of subst.

which is what we wanted to show

Case

\mathcal{D}:\Theta;\Delta;\Gamma\vdash\Lbag e\mathrel{;}\theta\Rbag:{T}\rightsquigarrow\llbracket\rho_{2}\rrbracket E/\Theta_{2};\rho_{2}\circ\rho_{1}

\Theta;\Delta;\Gamma\vdash\Lbag e\mathrel{;}\theta\Rbag\rightsquigarrow E{\,\mathrel{:}\,}T_{1}/\Theta_{1};\rho_{1}

\Theta_{1};\llbracket\rho_{1}\rrbracket\Delta\vdash T_{1}\doteq\llbracket\rho_{1}\rrbracket T/\Theta_{2};\rho_{2}

by assumption

for any grounding inst.
\rho_{g}
we have

\llbracket\rho_{o}\rrbracket\Delta;\llbracket\rho_{0}\rrbracket\Gamma\vdash\llbracket\rho_{g}\rrbracket E\mathrel{\Rightarrow}\llbracket\rho_{g}\rrbracket T_{1}

by i.h. (2) where
\rho_{o}=\rho_{g}\circ\rho_{1}

(\dagger)

for any grounding inst.
\rho_{g}^{\prime}
we have

\llbracket\rho_{g}^{\prime}\circ\rho_{2}\rrbracket T_{1}=\llbracket\rho_{g}^{\prime}\circ\rho_{2}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket T

by prop of unification and applying a grounding subst
(\ddagger)

\llbracket\rho_{g}^{\prime}\circ\rho_{2}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket\Delta;\llbracket\rho_{g}^{\prime}\circ\rho_{2}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket\Gamma\vdash\llbracket\rho_{g}^{\prime}\circ\rho_{2}\rrbracket E\mathrel{\Rightarrow}\llbracket\rho_{g}^{\prime}\circ\rho_{2}\rrbracket T_{1}

from
(\dagger)
using
\rho_{g}=\rho_{g}^{\prime}\circ\rho_{2}

\llbracket\rho_{g}^{\prime}\circ\rho_{2}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket\Delta;\llbracket\rho_{g}^{\prime}\circ\rho_{2}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket\Gamma\vdash\llbracket\rho_{g}^{\prime}\circ\rho_{2}\rrbracket E\mathrel{\Rightarrow}\llbracket\rho_{g}^{\prime}\circ\rho_{2}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket T

by
(\ddagger)

\llbracket\rho_{g}^{\prime}\circ\rho_{2}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket\Delta;\llbracket\rho_{g}^{\prime}\circ\rho_{2}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket\Gamma\vdash\llbracket\rho_{g}^{\prime}\circ\rho_{2}\rrbracket E\mathrel{\Leftarrow}\llbracket\rho_{g}^{\prime}\circ\rho_{2}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket T

by
t-syn

which is what we wanted to show

For(2):

Case

\mathcal{E}:\Theta;\Delta;\Gamma\vdash\Lbag e\,[c]\mathrel{;}\theta\Rbag\rightsquigarrow E_{1}\,[C]{\,\mathrel{:}\,}[C/X](\llbracket\rho_{2}\rrbracket T)/\Theta_{2};\rho_{2}\circ\rho_{1}

\Theta;\Delta;\Gamma\vdash\Lbag e\mathrel{;}\theta\Rbag\rightsquigarrow E_{1}{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\Pi^{e}X{\,\mathrel{:}\,}U.\,T/\Theta_{1};\rho_{1}

\Theta_{1};\llbracket\rho_{1}\rrbracket\Delta\vdash\Lbag c\mathrel{;}\llbracket\rho_{1}\rrbracket\theta\Rbag:{U}\rightsquigarrow C/\Theta_{2};\rho_{2}

by assumption

for any grounding instantiation
\rho_{g}

\cdot\vdash\rho_{g}{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\Theta_{1}

(\dagger)

for any grounding instantiation
\rho_{g}^{\prime}

by soundness of index reconstruction

\llbracket\rho_{g}^{\prime}\circ\rho_{2}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket\Delta;\llbracket\rho_{g}^{\prime}\circ\rho_{2}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket\Gamma\vdash\llbracket\rho_{g}^{\prime}\circ\rho_{2}\rrbracket E_{1}\mathrel{\Rightarrow}\llbracket\rho_{g}^{\prime}\circ\rho_{2}\rrbracket\Pi^{e}X{\,\mathrel{:}\,}U.\,T

(\dagger)
\cdot\vdash\rho_{g}{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\Theta_{1}
so we can instantiate

\llbracket\rho_{g}^{\prime}\circ\rho_{2}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket\Delta;\llbracket\rho_{g}^{\prime}\circ\rho_{2}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket\Gamma\vdash\llbracket\rho_{g}^{\prime}\circ\rho_{2}\rrbracket E_{1}\mathrel{\Rightarrow}\Pi^{e}X{\,\mathrel{:}\,}(\llbracket\rho_{g}^{\prime}\circ\rho_{2}\rrbracket U).\,(\llbracket\rho_{g}^{\prime}\circ\rho_{2}\rrbracket T)

by properties of substitutions

\llbracket\rho_{g}^{\prime}\circ\rho_{2}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket\Delta;\llbracket\rho_{g}^{\prime}\circ\rho_{2}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket\Gamma\vdash(\llbracket\rho_{g}^{\prime}\circ\rho_{2}\rrbracket E_{1})\,\llbracket\rho_{g}^{\prime}\rrbracket C\mathrel{\Rightarrow}[\llbracket\rho_{g}^{\prime}\rrbracket C\}/X](\llbracket\rho_{g}^{\prime}\circ\rho_{2}\rrbracket T)

by
t-app-index

\llbracket\rho_{g}^{\prime}\circ\rho_{2}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket\Delta;\llbracket\rho_{g}^{\prime}\circ\rho_{2}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket\Gamma\vdash\llbracket\rho_{g}^{\prime}\rrbracket((\llbracket\rho_{2}\rrbracket E_{1})\,C)\mathrel{\Rightarrow}\llbracket\rho_{g}^{\prime}\rrbracket([C/X](\llbracket\rho_{2}\rrbracket T)

by properties of substitutions

which is what we wanted to show.

Case

\mathcal{E}:\Theta;\Delta;\Gamma\vdash\Lbag x\mathrel{;}\theta\Rbag\rightsquigarrow E_{1}{\,\mathrel{:}\,}T_{1}~/~\Theta_{1};{\texttt{id}}(\Theta_{1})

by assumption

\Delta;\Gamma\vdash x\mathrel{\Rightarrow}T

by rule
t-var
(\dagger)

for any grounding inst.
\rho_{g}
s.t.
\cdot\vdash\Theta_{1}
we have:

\llbracket\rho_{g}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket\Delta;\llbracket\rho_{g}\circ\rho_{1}\rrbracket\Gamma\vdash\llbracket\rho_{g}\rrbracket E_{1}{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\llbracket\rho_{g}\rrbracket T_{1}

by
(\dagger)
, weakening and lemma
1
with
\rho_{1}={\texttt{id}}(\Theta_{1})

which is what we wanted to show

For (3):

Case

\mathcal{F}:\Delta;\Gamma\vdash\Lbag pat\mapsto e\mathrel{;}\theta\Rbag:{S\to T}\rightsquigarrow\Pi\Delta_{r};\Gamma_{r}.Pat^{\prime}{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\theta\mapsto E

\Delta\vdash pat:{S}\rightsquigarrow\Pi\Delta_{r};\Gamma_{r}.Pat{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\theta_{r}\mid\theta_{e}

by assumption

\cdot;\cdot\vdash pat\rightsquigarrow Pat:S^{\prime}/\Theta_{p};\Delta_{p};\Gamma_{p}\mid\cdot

\Delta_{p}^{\prime}\vdash\rho:\Theta_{p}
and

\Gamma_{r}=[\theta_{p}]\llbracket\rho\rrbracket\Gamma_{p}
,
Pat^{\prime}=[\theta_{p}]\llbracket\rho\rrbracket Pat

by inversion on
el-subst

\Delta^{\prime}_{p},\llbracket\rho\rrbracket\Delta_{p};\llbracket\rho\rrbracket\Gamma_{p}\vdash\llbracket\rho\rrbracket Pat\mathrel{\Leftarrow}\llbracket\rho\rrbracket S^{\prime}

by pattern elaboration lemma

\Delta,\Delta^{\prime}_{p},\llbracket\rho\rrbracket\Delta_{p}\vdash\llbracket\rho\rrbracket S^{\prime}\doteqdot S/\Delta_{r},\theta

by inversion on
el-subst

where we can split
\theta
as
\theta=\theta_{r},\theta_{i},\theta_{e}
so that:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\Delta_{r}\vdash\theta_{r}{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\Delta\\
\Delta_{r}\vdash\theta_{i}{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\Delta^{\prime}_{p}\\
\Delta_{r}\vdash\theta_{i},\theta_{e}{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\Delta^{\prime}_{p},\llbracket\rho\rrbracket\Delta_{p}\end{array}\right.

let
\theta_{p}=\theta_{i},\theta_{e}

[\underbrace{\theta_{i},\theta_{e}}_{\theta_{p}}]\llbracket\rho\rrbracket S^{\prime}=[\theta_{r}]S

by soundness of unification and the fact that
\Delta
and

\Delta^{\prime}_{p},\llbracket\rho\rrbracket\Delta_{p}
are distinct

\Delta_{r};[\theta_{p}]\llbracket\rho\rrbracket\Gamma_{p}\vdash[\theta_{p}]\llbracket\rho\rrbracket Pat\mathrel{\Leftarrow}[\theta_{p}]\llbracket\rho\rrbracket S^{\prime}

by substitution lemma

\Delta_{r};\underbrace{[\theta_{p}]\llbracket\rho\rrbracket\Gamma_{p}}_{\Gamma_{r}}\vdash\underbrace{[\theta_{p}]\llbracket\rho\rrbracket Pat}_{Pat^{\prime}}\mathrel{\Leftarrow}[\theta_{r}]S

by
[\theta]\llbracket\rho\rrbracket S^{\prime}=[\theta_{r}]S

\cdot;\Delta_{r};[\theta_{r}]\Gamma,\Gamma_{r}\vdash\Lbag e\mathrel{;}\theta_{r}\circ\theta,~\theta_{e}\Rbag:{[\theta_{r}]T}\rightsquigarrow E/\cdot;\cdot

by assumption

\Delta_{r};[\theta_{r}]\Gamma,\Gamma_{r}\vdash E\mathrel{\Leftarrow}[\theta_{r}]T

by (1)

\Delta;\Gamma\vdash\Pi\Delta_{r};\Gamma_{r}.Pat^{\prime}{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\theta_{r}\mapsto E\mathrel{\Leftarrow}S\to T

by
t-branch

which is what we wanted to show.

∎

Chapter 7
Babybel’s Translation Meta-theory

Lemma 3

(Ambient Context)
.

If
\Gamma({\texttt{u}})=[\Psi\vdash\textbf{a}]
then

\ulcorner\Gamma\urcorner(u)={\texttt{sftm}}[\ulcorner\Psi\urcorner,\textbf{a}]
.

Proof.

Induction on the structure of
\Gamma
.
∎

Lemma 4

(Terms)
.

1.

If
\Gamma;\Psi\vdash M{\,\mathrel{:}\,}A
then
\cdot;\ulcorner\Gamma\urcorner\vdash\ulcorner M\urcorner_{\Psi\vdash A}{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\ulcorner\Psi\vdash A\urcorner
.

2.

If
\Gamma;\Psi\vdash\sigma{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\Phi
then
\cdot;\ulcorner\Gamma\urcorner\vdash\ulcorner\sigma\urcorner_{\Psi\vdash\Phi}:\ulcorner\Psi\vdash\Phi\urcorner

Proof.

Induction on the typing derivation.

Case

\mathcal{D}=\Gamma;\Psi\vdash x{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\textbf{a}\Psi(x)=\textbf{a}

\cdot;\ulcorner\Gamma\urcorner\vdash{\texttt{Var}}[\ulcorner\Psi\urcorner,~\textbf{a}]~k:{\texttt{sftm}}[\ulcorner\Psi\urcorner,~\textbf{a}]
by the correctness of our translation function that computes the position
k
of
x
in
\Psi
.

\cdot;\ulcorner\Gamma\urcorner\vdash\ulcorner x\urcorner_{\Psi\vdash\textbf{a}}:\ulcorner\Psi\vdash\textbf{a}\urcorner
by definition

Case

\mathcal{D}=\Gamma;\Phi\vdash M[\sigma]^{\Phi}_{\Psi}:A\lx@proof@logical@and\Gamma;\Psi\vdash M{\,\mathrel{:}\,}A\Gamma;\Phi\vdash\sigma{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\Psi

\cdot;\ulcorner\Gamma\urcorner\vdash\ulcorner M\urcorner_{\Psi\vdash A}{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\ulcorner\Psi\vdash A\urcorner
by i.h.

\cdot;\ulcorner\Gamma\urcorner\vdash\ulcorner\sigma\urcorner_{\Phi\vdash\Psi}{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\ulcorner\Phi\vdash\Psi\urcorner
by i.h.

e={\texttt{apply\_sub}}~\ulcorner M\urcorner_{\Psi\vdash A}~\ulcorner\sigma\urcorner_{\Phi\vdash\Psi}
and

\cdot;\ulcorner\Gamma\urcorner\vdash e:\ulcorner\Phi\vdash A\urcorner
by property of
apply_sub

Case

\mathcal{D}=\Gamma;\Psi\vdash{\texttt{'u}}{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\textbf{a}\Gamma({\texttt{u}})=[\Psi\vdash\textbf{a}]

\ulcorner\Gamma\urcorner({\texttt{u}})={\texttt{sftm}}[\ulcorner\Psi,\textbf{a}\urcorner]
by
Lemma
3

\cdot;\ulcorner\Gamma\urcorner\vdash\ulcorner{\texttt{'u}}\urcorner{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\ulcorner\Psi\vdash\textbf{a}\urcorner

by rule
g-var
and definition

Case

\mathcal{D}=\Gamma;\Psi\vdash\lambda\,{x}.M{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\textbf{a}\to A\Gamma;\Psi,x{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\textbf{a}\vdash M{\,\mathrel{:}\,}A

\cdot;\ulcorner\Gamma\urcorner\vdash\ulcorner M\urcorner_{\Psi,\textbf{a}\vdash A}{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\ulcorner\Psi,\textbf{a}\vdash A\urcorner
by i.h.

\ulcorner\Psi\vdash\textbf{a}\to A\urcorner={\texttt{sftm}}[\ulcorner\Psi\urcorner,\ulcorner\textbf{a}\to A\urcorner]={\texttt{sftm}}[\ulcorner\Psi\urcorner,{\texttt{arr}}[\textbf{a},\ulcorner A\urcorner]]
by definition

\cdot;\ulcorner\Gamma\urcorner\vdash{\texttt{Lam~}}[{\texttt{cons~}}[\ulcorner\Psi\urcorner,\textbf{a},\ulcorner A\urcorner~]\,]~\ulcorner M\urcorner_{\Gamma,\textbf{a}\vdash A}:\ulcorner\Psi\vdash\textbf{a}\to A\urcorner
by using
g-con

Similar for the other cases.

∎

Lemma 5

(Pat.)
.

If
\vdash pat{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\tau\mathrel{\downarrow}\Gamma
then
\cdot\vdash\ulcorner pat\urcorner_{\Psi\vdash A}^{\Gamma}{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\ulcorner\tau\urcorner\mathrel{\downarrow}{\Gamma}
.

Proof.

By induction on the type derivation for patterns.

∎

Lemma 6

(Ctx. Pat.)
.

If
\Psi\vdash R{\,\mathrel{:}\,}A\mathrel{\downarrow}\Gamma
then
\cdot\vdash\ulcorner R\urcorner_{\Psi\vdash A}^{\Gamma}{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\ulcorner\Psi\vdash A\urcorner\mathrel{\downarrow}{\Gamma}
.

Proof.

By induction on the typing derivation. The interesting case is the one where
R
is a pattern variable.

Case:

\mathcal{D}=\Psi\vdash{\texttt{'u}}{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\textbf{a}\mathrel{\downarrow}{u{\,\mathrel{:}\,}[\Psi\vdash\textbf{a}]}

\cdot\vdash u{\,\mathrel{:}\,}{\texttt{sftm}}[\ulcorner\Psi\urcorner,\textbf{a}]\mathrel{\downarrow}{\cdot;u{\,\mathrel{:}\,}{\texttt{sftm}}[\ulcorner\Psi\urcorner,\textbf{a}]}
by
gp-var

\cdot\vdash\ulcorner{\texttt{'u}}\urcorner^{u{\,\mathrel{:}\,}[\Psi\vdash\textbf{a}]}_{\textbf{a}}{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\ulcorner\Psi\vdash\textbf{a}\urcorner\mathrel{\downarrow}{\cdot;\ulcorner u{\,\mathrel{:}\,}[\Psi\vdash\textbf{a}]\urcorner}

by definition

The other cases are similar.

∎

Theorem 7..1

(Main)
.

1.

If
\Gamma\vdash e\mathrel{\Leftarrow}\tau
then
\cdot;\ulcorner\Gamma\urcorner\vdash\ulcorner e\urcorner_{\Gamma\vdash\tau}{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\ulcorner\tau\urcorner
.

2.

If
\Gamma\vdash i\mathrel{\Rightarrow}\tau
then
\cdot;\ulcorner\Gamma\urcorner\vdash\ulcorner i\urcorner_{\Gamma\vdash\tau}{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\ulcorner\tau\urcorner
.

Proof.

By mutual induction on the type derivations.

Case

\mathcal{D}=\Gamma\vdash[\hat{\Psi}\vdash M]\mathrel{\Leftarrow}[\Psi\vdash\textbf{a}]\Gamma;\Psi\vdash M{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\textbf{a}

\cdot;\ulcorner\Gamma\urcorner\vdash\ulcorner M\urcorner_{\Psi\vdash\textbf{a}}{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\ulcorner\Psi\vdash\textbf{a}\urcorner
from Lemma
4
.

\ulcorner\Psi\vdash\textbf{a}\urcorner={\texttt{sftm}}[\ulcorner\Psi\urcorner,\ulcorner A\urcorner]
and
\ulcorner\Psi\vdash\textbf{M}\urcorner_{\Psi\vdash\textbf{a}}=\ulcorner M\urcorner_{\Psi\vdash\textbf{a}}

\ulcorner\Gamma\urcorner\vdash\ulcorner\hat{\Psi}\vdash M]\urcorner_{\Psi\vdash\textbf{a}}{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\ulcorner[\Psi\vdash\textbf{a}]\urcorner
by definition

Case

\mathcal{D}=\Gamma\vdash{\texttt{cmatch}}\,i\,{\texttt{with}}\ \overrightarrow{b}\mathrel{\Leftarrow}\tau\lx@proof@logical@and\Gamma\vdash i\mathrel{\Rightarrow}[\Psi\vdash\textbf{a}]\forall b\in\overrightarrow{b}\mathrel{.}\Gamma\vdash b\mathrel{\Leftarrow}[\Psi\vdash\textbf{a}]\to\tau

We note that each
b_{i}\in\overrightarrow{b}
is of the form
[\Psi\vdash R]\mapsto e
.

\Psi\vdash R{\,\mathrel{:}\,}A\mathrel{\downarrow}\Gamma

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash e\mathrel{\Leftarrow}\tau
by typing inversion

\cdot\vdash\ulcorner R\urcorner^{\Gamma^{\prime}}_{\Psi\vdash\textbf{a}}{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\textbf{a}\mathrel{\downarrow}\Gamma^{\prime}
by Lemma
6

\cdot;\ulcorner\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\urcorner\vdash\ulcorner e\urcorner_{\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\tau}{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\ulcorner\tau\urcorner
by i.h. (1).

\cdot;\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\ulcorner R\urcorner^{\Gamma^{\prime}}_{\Psi\vdash\textbf{a}}\mapsto\ulcorner e\urcorner_{\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}}{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\textbf{a}\to\ulcorner\tau\urcorner
by
g-branch

\cdot;\ulcorner\Gamma\urcorner\vdash\ulcorner i\urcorner_{\Gamma\vdash\textbf{a}}{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\textbf{a}
by I.H (2).

\cdot;\ulcorner\Gamma\urcorner\vdash\ulcorner{\texttt{cmatch}}\,i\,{\texttt{with}}\ \overrightarrow{b}\urcorner_{\Gamma\vdash\tau}:\ulcorner\tau\urcorner
by
g-match

Case

\mathcal{D}=\Gamma\vdash i\mathrel{\Leftarrow}\tau\Gamma\vdash i\mathrel{\Rightarrow}\tau^{\prime}\qquad\tau=\tau^{\prime}

\cdot;\ulcorner\Gamma\urcorner\vdash\ulcorner i\urcorner_{\Gamma\vdash\tau}:\ulcorner\tau\urcorner
by i.h.(2)

The other cases for part 1) are similar.

Case

\mathcal{D}=\Gamma\vdash x\mathrel{\Rightarrow}\tau\Gamma(x)=\tau

\cdot;\ulcorner\Gamma\urcorner\vdash\ulcorner x\urcorner_{\Gamma\vdash\tau}:\ulcorner\tau\urcorner
trivial using
g-var
.

Case

\mathcal{D}=\Gamma\vdash{i}\,{e}\mathrel{\Rightarrow}\tau\lx@proof@logical@and\Gamma\vdash i\mathrel{\Rightarrow}\tau^{\prime}\to\tau\Gamma\vdash e\mathrel{\Leftarrow}\tau^{\prime}

\cdot;\ulcorner\Gamma\urcorner\vdash\ulcorner i\urcorner_{\Gamma\vdash\tau^{\prime}\to\tau}{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\ulcorner\tau^{\prime}\to\tau\urcorner
by i.h.

\cdot;\ulcorner\Gamma\urcorner\vdash\ulcorner i\urcorner_{\Gamma\vdash\tau^{\prime}\to\tau}{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\ulcorner\tau^{\prime}\urcorner\to\ulcorner\tau\urcorner
by definition

\cdot;\ulcorner\Gamma\urcorner\vdash\ulcorner e\urcorner_{\Gamma\vdash\tau^{\prime}}{\,\mathrel{:}\,}\ulcorner\tau^{\prime}\urcorner
by i.h.

\cdot;\ulcorner\Gamma\urcorner\vdash\ulcorner{i}\,{e}\urcorner_{\Gamma\vdash\tau}:\ulcorner\tau\urcorner
by
g-app

The other cases for part 2) are similar.

∎

Chapter 8
A Beluga Implementation of Orca’s Translation Example

This chapter contains an implementation of the example from
Section
2
written using the Beluga language. We do
not explain this example in detail, but it is here as a reference to
show how similar the two languages are.

We start by defining the languages and translation of types using the
logical framework LF, this is similar to the Orca implementation,
except that where Orca uses a function to implement the translation of
types here we have a relation that is inhabited by the pairs of source
types and their translations. Notice the schema declaration that
allows for storing blocks of assumptions in the context thus avoiding
the need to declare a context relation.

With these lemmas in place, the translation can be implemented, notice
how the context relation was made superfluous by the information
carried in the context schema. Context schemas for Orca remain an
unexplored subject.
